## RE2-COLLECT-006: stable conditions preserve departed pickups

Status: Confirmed for required-item publication, the two observed pickup identities, first-item departure/reentry, fresh-tracker reconstruction and the listed DuckStation restores in source `01-07` on SLUS-00421. Other generated identities and arbitrary flag conditions remain Candidate. Native opposite-scenario cases, all-collected ordinary departure/reentry and normal memory-card persistence remain unvalidated. Failed sufficient conditions stay Unknown and never establish absence.

Game: Resident Evil 2 USA Disc 1, Leon A, SLUS-00421, executable `SLUS_004.21`, SHA256 `1cd618bc4390db4bd60735b0db1e91534f6134ede56fae454eb580be2aa686ef`. DuckStation `0.1-12083-g00e299795`; runtime signature SHA256 `712628cd70ecee75e834248085df57ac0b22862fa8e7e486e06cc9833cae5d5a`. [RE2-IDENTITY-001](#re2-identity-001-leon-a-disc-identity-and-candidate-room-layout) pins identity. [RE2-COLLECT-005](#re2-collect-005-source-proven-pre-delivery-event-presence) supplies the bounded source proof and native scheduling/continuation contract.

### Defect and shared proof

Baseline `8103ebc` publishes ordinary pickups from their current active descriptors, acquisition bits or unconditional source proofs. It does not preserve a pickup declared only under a supported startup condition after departure. Earlier captured `01-07` observations have two active pickups but no unconditional proof. After collecting one and leaving, its other pickup becomes Unknown and the room becomes Unknown rather than Incomplete.

The existing bounded proof now accepts explicit startup-creator targets as well as scheduled-delivery targets. Both modes use the same traversal, supported effects, guarded path formulas and step/depth/call/formula limits. Both synchronous roots must finish through audited effects. Startup flag writes, unsupported continuation, task cancellation/chaining and fixed-slot replacement still withhold proof. Ordinary items additionally retain their existing slot/identity/type/reset exclusions. An unrelated conflicting slot cannot erase an independent identity, but a reliable path to a creator cannot override later repurposing of its slot. All earlier unconditional identities and event-award rules remain unchanged.

The revision profile now explicitly lists which flag predicates may become live presence conditions. The initial set is bank 1 bits 0, 1 and 6, fixed by this profile's scenario mask, and the bank 4 bit 7 story gate already tested in RE2-COLLECT-005. Both generation modes and the shared metadata validator use that same set. Other predicates are opaque and require both paths; they do not silently become unsupported live conditions. Profile loading requires a nonempty, unique, well-formed supported set and rejects metadata that references another predicate.

A discarded intermediate export treated bank 0 bit 25 as a live startup predicate. This is the lifecycle word at PS1 virtual `0x800C7CE4`, width four, unsigned little-endian, mask `0x00000040`, also the reader's required gameplay mask. In the preceding `active-initial` capture, ordinary ID 116 is available even though its creator's expected-zero startup condition disagrees with the live set bit. Source interpretation alone cannot justify reusing such a lifecycle value after initialization. The three intermediate bank-zero rules were removed before final validation. Regressions explicitly keep bank-zero, event-stop and unapproved input predicates opaque. No live lifecycle flag was patched to test the opposite branch.

Metadata adds an explicit `conditional_item_presence` list to every source. Each exported identity has either a required tag or bounded sufficient conditions. Empty lists explicitly mean no additional proof; a missing list is invalid configuration. Rules must name a uniquely typed catalog identity, cannot duplicate one another or an existing unconditional identity, and share event-award condition validation. The reader gives acquisition and current availability priority, then checks the existing unconditional proof or the new sufficient condition from fresh flags. A mismatch leaves Unknown. Evaluated flag words participate in the existing consumed-byte coherence checks. No item presence or completion is persisted with exploration.

Owned-disc regeneration adds twelve rules, ten for relevant collectibles, across `00-0f`, `01-02`, `01-03`, `01-07` and `01-08`. These include conditions that never match the pinned Leon A scenario and do not imply absence. All legacy room fields and event rules remain equal after removing the additional list and claim reference. Floor metadata stays byte-identical. These source counts are not gameplay coverage or readiness percentages.

### Native case and addresses

Owned `PL0/RDT/ROOM2070.RDT`, source `01-07`, SHA256 `07708bbe7b2c26b57dfb41a12324d90e0ada78ab0bac54df8ab88dd0d2355bbf`, declares ordinary type 38 pickups at zero-based RDT file offsets 4090 and 4150, global acquisition IDs 148 and 167, quantity one each, slots 5 and 6. Root `16:0` checks bank 1 bit 6 at 3854 and returns early when set. Its clear branch reaches both creators. Root `17:0` has a corresponding alternate startup branch, then finishes without changing the relevant identity. The sufficient condition for both pickups is bank 1 bit 6 clear. No room, item, slot or predicate identity is hardcoded into the proof engine.

Bank 1 is PS1 virtual `0x800C7D04`, width four, unsigned little-endian. Bits 0, 1 and 6 use masks `0x80000000`, `0x40000000` and `0x02000000`; together they form the pinned scenario mask `0xC2000000`, required value zero. Bank 4 bit 7 remains PS1 virtual `0x800CC980`, width four, unsigned little-endian, mask `0x01000000`. Acquisition ID 148 uses PS1 virtual `0x800CC9F8`, width four, unsigned little-endian, mask `0x00000800`; ID 167 uses `0x800CC9FC`, the same width/order, mask `0x01000000`. Set acquisition bits mean collected.

### Native observations and reproducible restores

Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-conditional-pickups-20261019/`. Boot uses the existing `key-used-unlocked.sav`, SHA256 `361435efcb9330a8fb1e4a712ec08b966eab81fe5c6e2448a4a5079f31b581e7`, in `01-04`. Position-assisted native doors lead through `01-05` to `01-07`. Both pickups initially publish Available. Slot/lifetime proof now establishes both as remaining independently of active descriptors after departure.

Native repeated Cross collects ID 148 from the corridor approach at X = -14500, Z = -11000, yaw zero. Its acquisition bit changes clear to set, and inventory gains one type-38 item. Ordinary passage back to `01-05` leaves ID 148 Taken and ID 167 Uncollected. The corrected room stays Incomplete; baseline `8103ebc` makes the second item Unknown and the room Unknown. Native reentry retains Taken/Available. A later earlier-state restore repeats native collection of ID 148, then the distinct approach X = -14500, Z = -9200, yaw zero collects ID 167. Both bits are set and inventory contains two type-38 pickups. The current room now reports Cleared from the resolved two-item list. These are three native collection operations on two identities, including the first identity's repeat after an earlier load.

The two trigger rectangles overlap. Earlier approaches inside nonwalkable geometry did not activate a pickup; native forward motion there was collision-limited. The initial second-item approach fell in the overlap and repeated ID 148 rather than establishing ID 167. An enemy-interrupted attempt likewise does not prove the second collection. `second-interrupted` was renamed from an overly optimistic provisional filename; its bits establish only the first item. The final `departed-all-attempt` remains in `01-07`, so it is excluded as all-collected passage/reentry evidence. The accepted first-item departure/reentry is separate. No lock, story, inventory or acquisition patch forces these outcomes.

Four captures return Retry and leave both baseline and corrected trackers unchanged: two door-transition/setup contexts and two pickup prompts. Prompt contexts report a relocated section layout that no longer matches the room source, as in preceding item validation. Those rejected reads are not accepted item publications or new room visits. The remaining nineteen captures publish successfully. The private audit compares every capture against both readers, checks the recorded map publication exactly, and verifies unchanged current pose, ordinary items outside the affected source, doors and visit sets.

Hashes cover full 2 MiB research captures outside Git. The production reader still uses bounded player/save blocks and consumed-byte coherence checks. The final private tracker contains exactly `01-04`, `01-05` and `01-07`, three sources and three map regions, all previously observed. No source extends the preceding twenty-seven-source runtime union. No user history is opened or extended.

Six actual native F1 loads are logged. The original first-collected checkpoint, `after-pickup.sav`, SHA256 `cbbb5830abfc4e95e89a34c84b731a58a77a1d4da836360b7b40913de1b71ed9`, restores one collection during an enemy interaction; it is not a settled navigation checkpoint. The uncollected `before-pickup.sav`, SHA256 `36ad24917c07fa69b0ced859e7f2efc88a6abae0a5569a881df50bb68ac91507`, supplies three byte-identical earlier restores. The native all-collected checkpoint, `all-collected.sav`, SHA256 `715a93961235cc64839eac03a3e0768f7ea367479b52cb9480e300f917ff678d`, supplies two byte-identical later restores. The earlier states publish Available/Available and Incomplete; later states publish Taken/Taken and Cleared. Visits persist separately. Independent fresh trackers seeded only from the actual accepted `room-ready` visit reconstruct the earlier, later and departed-first outcomes from fresh captures without retained item conclusions.

The native case's clear scenario gate is mandatory for Leon A, so an opposite gate correctly rejects the profile rather than validating an alternate item branch. Synthetic false-gate regressions use an explicitly substituted mutable story predicate and remain separate from native evidence. Other conditional pickups, all-collected ordinary departure/reentry, uninterrupted ordinary navigation and normal memory-card saves are outside this confirmation.

### Isolation, verification and contributions

Both achievement integrations were verified disabled before boot and all six selected-slot loads. Memory cards were None, audio muted, per-game overrides absent and the existing black shader hash-checked. The private screenshot's central half was entirely black; no gameplay image was emitted. An empty test app was briefly opened and closed before the configured boot; it loaded no game or save and did not change the original settings bytes. The checkpoints retain inherited high health and the documented 255-round ammunition assistance. Observed health ranges from 27007 to 27667. This increment adds only recorded position/facing preparation, not health, ammunition, inventory, story, acquisition, floor or key patches. The position log lists every PS1 virtual address, width, prior bytes and replacement value. Coordinate fields are four-byte little-endian values with the existing two-byte collision aliases; yaw and aliases are two-byte little-endian. The existing repeated-Cross macro was used without changing its configuration.

After testing, the GDB controller and isolated app were stopped. Both temporary and canonical private-tmp executable paths were checked. Original settings and selected slot were restored byte for byte, SHA256 `792443989dc8f5f8ee3dabcc2a20dd71a3e61cc0a44de8a84266d51e6e889069` and `995bb0ff5df6eb6b254d87ea9c6af5386105a50bfd50cdab018da50465965bfa`. Proprietary sources, executable, RAM, checkpoints and private screenshots stay outside Git.

The final focused suite passes 243 tests. New regressions cover guarded and unconditional creators, complementary identities, opaque and volatile predicates, unsupported continuation, startup writes, asynchronous-only creators, slot/reset replacement, malformed/duplicate/unsupported metadata, missing required profile configuration, current/departed completion, false-gate uncertainty, fresh flag reversal and changed/short gate reads. The complete suite passes 1,872 tests in 111.87 seconds, exit zero. Scoped Ruff lint/format, all 133-outline RE2 browser checks, whitespace and byte-identical owned-disc regeneration pass. ## RE2-COLLECT-007: fixed-scenario proof excludes impossible pickups

Status: Confirmed for removal of ID 157's impossible check, collection of the two real pickups, all-collected ordinary departure/reentry and the listed DuckStation restores in source `00-0f` on SLUS-00421. ID 36's exclusion in `01-03` remains Candidate with source proof and earlier capture replay only. Other sources without a complete negative proof retain Unknown checks. No native opposite-scenario gameplay or normal memory-card persistence is confirmed.

Game: Resident Evil 2 USA Disc 1, Leon A, SLUS-00421, executable `SLUS_004.21`, SHA256 `1cd618bc4390db4bd60735b0db1e91534f6134ede56fae454eb580be2aa686ef`. Tested DuckStation `0.1-12083-g00e299795`; runtime signature SHA256 `712628cd70ecee75e834248085df57ac0b22862fa8e7e486e06cc9833cae5d5a`. [RE2-IDENTITY-001](#re2-identity-001-leon-a-disc-identity-and-candidate-room-layout) pins the revision. [RE2-COLLECT-006](#re2-collect-006-stable-conditions-preserve-departed-pickups) supplies the existing sufficient presence conditions. This claim does not reinterpret their failure as absence.

### Defect and separate exclusion proof

Baseline `5e0e791` retains every relevant catalog identity as a completion check. In the observed `00-0f` state, two real pickups are available, while another creator requires bank 1 bit 1 set, contrary to the pinned Leon A mask. Its missing descriptor and false sufficient presence condition alone cannot establish absence. The baseline leaves its check Unknown after both real pickups are collected and keeps the room Unknown.

The new negative proof derives fixed predicates from the required runtime scenario mask, expected value and matching flag-bank address. For this revision the fixed values are bank 1 bits 0, 1 and 6 clear. Generation and profile loading share that derivation. The room document must declare the exact typed predicate list and an explicit exclusion list for every source. Missing, duplicate, unknown, ambiguous, incomplete or contradictory metadata fails profile loading. An overlapping sufficient presence rule is allowed only if every clause contradicts a fixed scenario predicate. An unconditional requirement cannot overlap an exclusion.

The proof requires complete candidate coverage and audits all decoded procedures, including uncalled procedures, before traversing every procedure entry. It accepts audited initializer effects, ordinary creators, non-item interactions, canonical returns and leaf calls. Any decoded flag write, reset, generic item interaction, unsupported handler/effect, task control, non-leaf call or noncanonical return withholds the entire source's exclusions. A raw fixed-flag writer candidate anywhere within an exact procedure extent also withholds proof, including bytes after an early return. The decoder supplies those extents rather than duplicating section-bound calculations.

The bounded graph overapproximates all creation paths. A single fixed predicate selects its known branch. Story, lifecycle and compound conditions take both paths. True IF frames remain until Else/Endif; false branches discard the frame. Stack underflow, depth above four, missing decoded commands or more than 4,096 steps withholds proof. Calls may return, and each callee is also an independent root. Every creator variant for an identity must be unreachable, and the identity must have one catalog type. No named room, pickup, slot or scenario-bit identity is hardcoded in the traversal.

The reader omits only exported impossible identities from item details and completion counts. Shared acquisition bits cannot make a forbidden creator possible; their set and clear states are both covered by synthetic regressions. A live available descriptor contradicting an exclusion returns Retry without publishing. The existing fresh scenario gate, source validation and consumed-byte coherence checks still apply before publication. All other item, event, visit, door and geometry behavior stays unchanged. No extra runtime script or full-RAM scan is added.

Owned-disc regeneration exports exactly two exclusions: `00-0f` ID 157 and `01-03` ID 36. Every legacy room field, unconditional proof, sufficient condition, event binding and map byte remains unchanged. Opposite-scenario candidates in `01-02` and `01-08` remain unproven because their sources contain effects outside the audited subset. Export counts do not establish gameplay coverage or readiness.

The first prototype scanned the entire RDT for writer bytes and produced no exclusions. In `ROOM10F0.RDT`, candidate byte patterns at offsets 22594, 43743, 46031, 46447 and 46847 lie outside the executable script sections, in other data. The exact init range is 1456 through 1755 and the main range is 1756 through 2179. Restricting the scan to parser-supplied procedure extents removes those unrelated matches while retaining rejection of undecoded bytes inside scripts. Synthetic camera-section and dead-script writer cases preserve this distinction. The broad scan is Rejected; the scoped scan replaces it with new source-bound evidence.

### Source identities and native contracts

Owned `PL0/RDT/ROOM10F0.RDT`, length 171484, SHA256 `36c6981d7ed1bc4453cc4a3f09d77111567ca4f1ca44b639651912ec937d161a`, declares ID 157, type 20, quantity 30, slot 2 at zero-based file offset 1844. IF 1836 checks bank 1 bit 1 expected one at 1840 and skips to 1906 when false. The two real identities are ID 139, type 30, quantity 3, slot 3 at 1692 and ID 38, type 31, quantity 1, slot 5 at 1776. Files do not block completion. `PL0/RDT/ROOM2030.RDT`, length 121804, SHA256 `4a6311f34cb1288ddf25b46123bbc2cbf938e746e358dbeb6d29da6385b189bc`, declares excluded ID 36, type 35, quantity 1, slot 1 at 2152. The outer fixed check at 1988 expects bank 1 bit 1 clear; its true branch reaches Else 2148 and skips that alternate creator to 2214. A nested lifecycle condition takes both paths and cannot authorize an exclusion by itself.

Bank 1 is PS1 virtual `0x800C7D04`, width four, unsigned little-endian. Bits 0, 1 and 6 use masks `0x80000000`, `0x40000000` and `0x02000000`, together `0xC2000000`, required value zero. Acquisition ID 139 uses PS1 virtual `0x800CC9F8`, width four, unsigned little-endian, mask `0x00100000`; ID 38 uses `0x800CC9EC`, the same width/order, mask `0x02000000`. ID 157 shares word `0x800CC9F8` with mask `0x00000004`. Set acquisition bits mean collected, but the excluded identity's bit is not used to establish presence. Slot 2's registry entry is PS1 virtual `0x800C66D0`, width four, little-endian pointer, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`; it stays null in the accepted current-room captures. Null alone remains insufficient proof.

Private `native_condition.py`, `native_branch.py` and `native_frames.py` execute pinned native handlers on copied RAM with synthetic task/scripts. The dispatch table is PS1 virtual `0x8009F94C`, entries four-byte little-endian pointers. IF handler `0x800501C0` pushes the false target. Flag-check handler `0x80050BF0` returns false for the copied clear bit and true for the copied set bit. Running the actual dispatcher from `0x8004FF98` verifies the false result executes its pop/skip at `0x8004FFF4..0x80050028`, reaching synthetic offset 12 with the frame removed; the true result reaches offset 8 with the frame retained. Else `0x80050200` pops one frame and adds its own relative target. Endif `0x8005023C` pops one frame and advances two bytes. The native save block remains unchanged in every case.

These private-copy experiments use context `0x801F0000`, script `0x801D0000` and stop address `0x801FF000`, all PS1 virtual. Context script and stack pointers at offsets `0x1C` and `0x140` are four-byte little-endian with full masks. Host memory API accesses use physical RAM offsets. The copied scenario word changes only inside Unicorn, never in the live guest. Numeric outcomes and harnesses are retained privately; executable bytes and disassembly are not distributed. These checks validate the listed opcode and dispatcher contracts, not actual gameplay in another scenario or execution of the real forbidden creator.

### Hidden native reproduction and observations

Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-scenario-exclusions-20261019/`. The isolated emulator boots the existing `primary-allowed-unlocked.sav`, SHA256 `d53939aa01ef1e52d9bfc25c6503dac35740ec77626291b8c5cd8160bedd304d`, in `00-09`. Position-assisted native doors lead through `00-0a` into `00-0f`. Inherited high health and 255-round assistance remain enabled. New direct guest writes are the seven recorded position/facing preparations only; no inventory, acquisition, scenario, story, key, control-word or floor writes are made. Enemy animation overwrote an initial doorway placement. That failed preparation does not prove passage. A later bounded write watch waits for observed native free control before placing, then removes itself before resuming. It never forces the control state.

The preparation control word is PS1 virtual `0x800C7D28`, width four, unsigned little-endian, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`; observed `0x00010001` admits placement. This is test synchronization, not a new tracker interpretation rule. `control-sync.jsonl` records two successful synchronization attempts. Position writes use signed little-endian X/Z at `0x800C7D5C`/`0x800C7D64`, width four, yaw at `0x800C7D9A`, width two, and collision-position aliases at `0x800C7D68`, `0x800C7D6C`, `0x800C7D6E`, `0x800C7D72`, `0x800C7E3C` and `0x800C7E3E`, width two, all full-width masks. `position-edits.jsonl` retains before/after values and source assertions.

Native repeated Cross from X -18000, Z -20200, yaw zero collects ID 139. The approach avoids the overlapping file/alternate-item rectangle. A separate X -20900, Z -24500, yaw zero approach collects ID 38. Both acquisition bits change clear to set; inventory contains the corresponding types. This pass verifies acquisition identity and completion, not an isolated inventory-quantity transaction. The repeated interaction sequence leaves type 30 quantity two in inventory, while source/live pickup metadata lists three. No quantity inference or inventory patch is used to force the result.

Ordinary native departure to `00-0a` and reentry into `00-0f` retain both Taken states and Cleared completion. The baseline retains an additional Unknown check in every accepted case, including after both pickups and departure. Transition captures return Retry and retain publication; their provisional filenames were changed to describe transitions rather than successful settled results. The subsequent accepted current-source bytes establish actual passage and reentry.

Each pair reproduces full RAM bytes exactly. Three fresh production trackers independently reconstruct earlier, later and departed-clear results from actual captures, without retaining item conclusions.

## RE2-COLLECT-008: scenario exclusions through audited script effects

Status: Confirmed for IDs 158 and 70 during two ordinary collections, departure/reentry and the listed savestate restores in `01-08` and `01-09` on SLUS-00421. ID 249 is also confirmed through three ordinary pickups, departure/reentry and restores in `01-02`. The third follow-up confirms full clearance in `01-09`. Remaining ID 159 and full clearance in `01-08` are unverified; unresolved ID 116 prevents a clearance claim in `01-02`. The separate quantity discrepancy is resolved within [RE2-QUANTITY-001's scope](#re2-quantity-001-native-pickup-award-amount-differs-from-catalog).

Game: Resident Evil 2 USA Disc 1, Leon A, SLUS-00421. Executable `SLUS_004.21` SHA256 `1cd618bc4390db4bd60735b0db1e91534f6134ede56fae454eb580be2aa686ef`; isolated DuckStation `0.1-12083-g00e299795`. [RE2-IDENTITY-001](#re2-identity-001-leon-a-disc-identity-and-candidate-room-layout) pins revision and source formats. This extends [RE2-COLLECT-007](#re2-collect-007-fixed-scenario-proof-excludes-impossible-pickups) without changing its original observations.

### Defect and bounded proof

The earlier exclusion proof rejects whole sources containing unrelated event effects, so impossible alternate-scenario pickups remain unresolved alongside real items. The shared proof now accepts the audited native effects while preserving the separation between presence and absence. It seeds every decoded procedure independently, treats a call as possibly returning and explores both outcomes of opaque predicates. It never treats an opaque story or actor condition as evidence of absence. All creators for an identity must remain unreachable under the fixed scenario, and any item reset for that identity withholds exclusion.

Scheduled execution must name an existing main procedure with the native `FF 18` task selector. Interaction dispatch must bind to known source-declared/reset kinds 0, 1, 2 or 5; missing or unsupported bindings withhold proof. Generic item interaction creators remain unsupported. A do/while procedure contributes every command as reachable, and must contain paired local loop bounds, a single decoded read-only predicate of the declared width and no IF, Else, Endif or Goto. Four-frame and 4,096-step limits withhold proof rather than truncate exploration.

Flag writes require a declared bank, an in-range bit and a native clear, set or toggle operation, values 0, 1 or 7. Comparing PS1 virtual word addresses and masks catches aliased banks. A raw fixed-bit writer scan remains bounded to the decoded procedure extents, including undecoded trailing script bytes. Camera, message, audio and item/door interaction effects also reject overlapping fixed status masks. `Work_set` rejects unsupported actor kinds, signed-negative object indices and enemy indices outside the audited 32-pointer table. These restrictions deliberately leave unsupported sources unresolved.

The profile's scenario word remains PS1 virtual `0x800C7D04`, unsigned little-endian width four, required mask `0xC2000000`, value zero. The native bank-pointer table is `0x8009FD38`, unsigned little-endian four-byte pointers with full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`. Direct variable setters address halfwords at `0x800CC918 + 2 * unsigned-byte index`, through `0x800CCB16`, separate from the fixed word on this revision. These are PS1 virtual addresses; private Unicorn host accesses use RAM offsets masked by `0x1FFFFF`.

Native opcode dispatch uses `0x8009F94C`, four-byte little-endian pointers. Inspected native ranges include flag mutation `0x80050C50..0x80050D0C`, direct variables `0x80050DB8..0x80050E24`, work selection `0x80051F84..0x8005204C`, actor-member addressing `0x80052330..0x800524B0`, resets `0x800517D4..0x80051844`, interaction dispatch `0x80051848..0x800518DC`, do/while `0x800506EC..0x800508B8`, and enemy-table search `0x8001A990..0x8001A9D4`. `Work_set` kind 3 reads a signed-byte index from the pointer table at `0x800C7F48`; kind 4 computes `0x800D8450 + 504 * signed-byte index`. Actor member/position writes use nonnegative structure offsets. Native code lacks an index guard, so the offline proof supplies one.

Audited camera commands `0x29/0x2A` affect bank 1 mask `0x00100100`; camera command `0x3C` affects `0x00000100`; audio command `0x59` affects `0x00000020`. Message `0x2B` affects bank 2's upper half using its unsigned little-endian halfword operand. Item interaction kind 2 sets bank 1 mask `0x00008000`; door kind 1 sets bank 2 mask `0xFF000000`. All masks refer to unsigned little-endian four-byte words. The proof checks intersection with fixed masks instead of assuming these bits are unrelated.

### Offline outcomes and native-copy experiments

All offsets below are zero-based RDT file offsets. SHA256 identifies the owned source, which remains outside Git.

| Source | Length | SHA256 | Newly excluded acquisition ID and creators |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ROOM2020.RDT`, `01-02` | 167836 | `6ee69fee262b4390ac5f4ecf36c54335cd7f6fa1b3b14af71720925bcb54e8a9` | 249 at 7236; bank 1 bit 1 requires one at 7194 |
| `ROOM2080.RDT`, `01-08` | 185264 | `7e341f8b33db336f41a61a859683c7dea6680735a3b9bc5d37673517202c4120` | 158 at 5328; bank 1 bit 0 requires one at 5286 |
| `ROOM2090.RDT`, `01-09` | 78944 | `852fcf2c2f26551d5903f2e74190190b9a91a6d3b2793a1cd5aa1d7f376cb982` | 70 at 3356 and 3850; fixed true branches for bit 1 clear skip both alternate creators |

The first two sources were the motivating cases. The same shared rule also proves the third offline; none uses a room or item-name exception. Existing exclusions 157 and 36 remain unchanged. Real item presence/collection rules, event award rules, room outlines and door markers are byte-identical. Unresolved neighbor ID 116 remains unresolved. A shared acquisition bit alone never establishes scenario absence.

Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-scripted-exclusions-20261022/`. `native_effects_checked.py` executes seven pinned native handlers under scenario seeds zero and `0xC2000000`, fourteen cases total. It covers an unrelated flag write, maximum-index direct variable set/copy, actor position/member writes, camera status and an item reset with a different identity. Each case reaches its native return, advances by the exact packet width and preserves the fixed mask. The reset also reproduces its expected descriptor kind and three payload halfwords. Full 2 MiB before/after comparisons retain changed RAM offsets and byte values in `native-effects-checked.json`. This is synthetic isolated handler execution, not gameplay or exhaustive operand coverage.

An initial write-tracing-hook variant fails with a Unicorn exception at return. The same fourteen cases pass without that hook, including a variant without any hooks. The accepted variant keeps only a code hook and compares memory snapshots. This establishes an instrumentation-dependent failure for these Unicorn 2.1.4 cases. It does not establish a general emulator defect or game failure. The failed harness is preserved privately and is not counted as passing evidence.

Owned-disc regeneration reproduces both metadata files byte for byte. Synthetic regressions cover unrelated/aliased flag writes, unsupported operations/banks/ranges, alternate creators, reset aliases, interaction bindings/status bits, signed actor indices, opaque predicates and malformed loop/control boundaries. Production reader/tracker tests cover current/departed rooms, unresolved neighbors, shared acquisition bits, live contradictions and acquisition reversal with a fresh tracker. These tests do not establish new campaign coverage.

### Runtime limits and contributions

The original temporary RE2 checkpoints were no longer present. A new isolated instance was prepared with separate in-memory agent histories, memory cards None, muted audio, no per-game overrides and the existing black shader. Both achievement integrations were checked disabled before each boot. Setup attempts remained outside an active player state and are excluded from gameplay evidence. Private checkpoint images were decoded only for menu-text recognition, without emitting game images. At completion of that offline pass, no health, position, inventory, scenario, story, acquisition or control-state writes had been made. The later native follow-up records its own preparation below.

The private function-key binding experiment also failed to establish active gameplay. The isolated emulator and GDB listener were stopped, and those input bindings were restored byte for byte. `cleanup-final.json` records the restored settings hash. No renderer changes, so the README screenshot is unchanged. Proprietary data, raw RAM, diagnostic helpers and RE2 checkpoints remain outside Git. New runtime confirmation and the Opus perspective remain outstanding. The later human exception recorded below permits this increment to be committed with the completed Gemini review alone; it does not establish gameplay confirmation.

Contributions:

- 2026-10-22; human user; approved the named scripted-room scenario-exclusion increment and requested continuation. Gameplay must stay hidden and chat reports spoiler-free.
- 2026-10-22; Codex root, exact verified model `gpt-6.1-sol`; extended the conservative source proof, audited native effects/operand bounds and all source creators, regenerated metadata, added synthetic regressions, ran isolated native-copy experiments and recorded the failed tracing variant and runtime limits. Exact author identity is retained in private `author.json`.

- 2026-10-22; `gpt-6.1-sol`; the worktree suite passed 2,003 tests in 170.62 seconds. #### Preparation, rejected attempts and limits

Both achievement integrations were checked disabled before each boot and selected-slot load; per-game overrides were absent. No game image was emitted, and the user's exploration histories were never opened.

Agent assistance set signed little-endian 16-bit health at PS1 virtual `0x800C7E7A` to 30000, full mask `0xFFFF`. Recorded position preparations assert the current source and wait for the game's native free-control word `0x00010001` at `0x800C7D28`, unsigned little-endian width four, without writing that word. Signed little-endian X/Z at `0x800C7D5C`/`0x800C7D64`, width four, yaw at `0x800C7D9A`, width two, and collision-position aliases `0x800C7D68`, `0x800C7D6C`, `0x800C7D6E`, `0x800C7D72`, `0x800C7E3C`, `0x800C7E3E`, width two, use full-width write masks. `health-edits.jsonl`, `position-edits.jsonl` and `control-sync.jsonl` retain the writes and synchronization results. No ammunition, inventory, acquisition, key, scenario, story, floor, height or control-state patch was enabled. Doorways and stairs perform the native transitions.

Idle-demo startup samples and `active-setup-probe` are excluded from gameplay evidence. The `second-passage` capture is a transitional Retry. `target-one-reentered`, SHA256 `46e1a5ead6eb7273ae338cf1c68cb470a5a91833440847bd96310a9d00ce1ff0`, actually remains in `01-00`; its provisional name does not establish reentry. One source has no reverse door, and identical authored reverse poses are deduplicated rather than treated as distinct positions. No generic navigation rule is confirmed.

Discrete menu inputs with an unbound interval after release finish one prompt that earlier attempts left active. Further menu trials do not complete the access interaction or reach `01-08`/`01-09`. Private savestate-thumbnail and VRAM OCR experiments supply no additional gameplay outcome. #### Navigation, rejected approaches and cleanup

The first CLI attempt incorrectly supplied a filename to `-state`, which accepts a slot index; it exited before game load. The pinned executable's own help identifies `-statefile` as the filename option, and that launch succeeds. Before each boot/load, both achievement integrations are disabled and per-game overrides absent. The private empty-card path is confined to this pass; no user card/history is opened. Audio remains muted and the shader hash is unchanged from the preceding follow-up. A suppressed screenshot's central half is checked black without emitting an image. The starting native checkpoint retains earlier health assistance; this pass adds only recorded position/facing writes using the same whitelist and native free-control synchronization. No health, ammunition, enemy, inventory, item, key, scenario, story, floor, height or control-state value is patched.

Initial menu trials still target the wrong interaction. Read-only Down/Up observations move a candidate inventory cursor 0 → 2 → 0 at `0x800CDC7C`, unsigned byte, full mask `0xFF`; pinned native references establish the status base `0x800CDC70`. The proposed transfer of the later-release status address remains rejected. Native menu dispatch at `0x8006663C..0x80066714` sends action 3 to Use/Equip at `0x800667CC` and action 2 to Inspect at `0x80067A80`. An initial hypothesis reversing those actions is rejected. The earlier failure was a different world interaction/floor context, not an established menu-mapping defect.

The recorded [RE2-DOOR-001 approach](#re2-door-001-event-driven-unlock-and-savestate-reversal), native Y/floor supplied by `first-target-departed.sav`, X/Z -14935/2099 and yaw 2048, completes the access interaction through repeated Cross. Native stairs then change Y/floor before the route continues. Pickup preparation in `01-08` also requires approaching the free side of counter collision: native interaction code `0x8004DB34..0x8004DBBC` constructs a 620-unit forward point. Accepted settled positions are -19094/-16920, yaw 1024 for ID 141 and -15676/-17560, yaw 0 for ID 216, both on native floor zero. The native acquisition flags, rather than an approach position or elapsed time, establish collection.

`second-target-first-collected` is a failed approach with both acquisition flags clear. `second-first-reentered` remains in `01-07` after enemy contact; it supplies no successful reentry. Later captures verify the actual destination. Enemy contact, collision resolution, unsupported source geometry and a catalog route alone do not establish passage or item availability. Private `menu-diagnosis.json`, `key-route.json`, `control-sync.jsonl` and the native disassemblies retain rejected hypotheses and successful preparation separately.

The remaining `01-09` case has not been reached. Its route from `01-07` names key type 90. The short catalog key location in `00-13` is gated at owned RDT offsets 3874/3878 on bank 1 bit 1 set, while Leon A requires that bit clear. That local branch observation is a navigation rejection, not a new complete-source exclusion rule. The alternate creator in `00-0b` is gated at 5836/5840 on that bit clear, but its native route and pickup remain untested here. No key or progression state is patched to bypass the requirement.

After the final route restoration, the isolated process and GDB listener are stopped. Original settings and selected slot are restored byte for byte with the same hashes as the preceding follow-up; `cleanup.json` records both. No host private-card file exists at exit, and no normal memory-card persistence is claimed. Raw RAM, owned data, checkpoints, images and helpers remain private. No production or renderer change is made; the README screenshot is unchanged by this work. Remaining limits include item 159/full clearance, ID 70, ID 249's ordinary pickup/reentry checks and the separately reproduced quantity defect.

Reproduction and rejected preparation:

Delayed resume allows native input to be held before nearby actors can interrupt the return. It does not write the control word. ## RE2-COLLECT-009: native pickup and use keep acquisition identities separate

Status: Confirmed for the observed ID 209 pickup, ordinary departure/reentry, two native uses and the four stated restore pairs on SLUS-00421. ID 159 activation and full clearance remain unverified. The source's fifty-interaction threshold has one native activation sequence, so its general activation rule remains Candidate.

It establishes no ordinary route to the item. Its normal-play relevance and the unused-declaration hypothesis remain unresolved.

Owned source `ROOM1150.RDT`, source `00-15`, is 347152 bytes with SHA256 `ed5c71d21cdbf73c2e29ca33a23ea0c4bf09608217a1101e62f33ab4ccdba942`. Its type 80 creator at file offset 6552 uses slot 29 and acquisition ID 209. Generic interaction slot 6 at offset 6528 dispatches procedure `17:14`, whose entry is 6654. That procedure increments script variable 7, compares it with fifty at 6684, invokes `Aot_on` slot 29 at 6690 and assigns 51 at 6692. These are offsets in the owned RDT, not PS1 virtual addresses.

The native interaction reaches counter values zero, thirty and 51. At the activation prompt ID 209 is still clear and inventory contains no type 80. Further native confirmation collects one unit and sets ID 209. The counter reaches 57 during repeated confirmation, then resets to zero after ordinary reentry. It is not a durable acquisition record. The original earlier checkpoint reverses acquisition and inventory; the later checkpoint restores both. Each actual F1 load reproduces its reference RAM byte for byte.

All addresses in this entry are PS1 virtual. The counter is unsigned little-endian width two at `0x800CC926`, full mask `0xFFFF`. ID 209 uses unsigned little-endian width four at `0x800CCA00`, mask `0x00004000`; the word changes from `0x00000080` to `0x00004080`. ID 159 uses `0x800CC9F8`, same width/order, mask `0x00000001`, and stays clear throughout. Slot 29's pointer at `0x800C673C`, unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`, continues to identify type 80 and ID 209. Its descriptor SCE byte changes from 2 to zero after collection.

The real inventory item is then carried through native doors and stairs to `01-08`. Owned `ROOM2080.RDT` is 185264 bytes with SHA256 `7e341f8b33db336f41a61a859683c7dea6680735a3b9bc5d37673517202c4120`. This source's type 80 creator at offset 5384 uses slot 14 and ID 159. Sharing an item type does not share the acquisition ID. The prior generic-message attempts did not have this newly acquired inventory item and remain separate failed activation evidence.

Native inventory Use consumes type 80. Completing the resulting file display sets ID 160 at `0x800CC9FC`, unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0x80000000`. The intermediate captures already have an empty inventory slot but this bit is still clear; both readers return Retry and preserve their prior publication. The settled observation has ID 160 set, ID 209 still set and ID 159 still clear. A second native use after the earlier restore repeats those outcomes. Source inspection connects Use ID 80 to procedure `17:12` and `Aot_on` slot 16. The slot 16 descriptor identifies file type 123 and ID 160, changing from SCE 2 before use to zero afterward. Files remain outside clearance requirements under RE2-SCD-001.

The eight inventory records begin at `0x800CCB68`, four bytes per record; type and quantity are unsigned one-byte fields at offsets zero and one, each mask `0xFF`. Type 80 occupies slot 5 before use and disappears after it. Other records remain type/quantity `2/18`, `1/1`, `30/2`, `20/15` and `71/1`. ID 209 remains Taken even after inventory consumption and departure. ID 159 remains Available in its current source and source-established Uncollected after departure, keeping `01-08` Incomplete. Neither collection elsewhere nor file delivery establishes ID 159 collection or impossibility.

### Bounded dispatch follow-up, 2026-10-22

Status: Confirmed for the bounded message dispatch, unchanged acquisition, ordinary departure/reentry and checkpoint comparisons below on the same SLUS-00421 executable and DuckStation version. The inference that ID 159 is an unused declaration remains Candidate. This pass establishes no native activation, pickup, item-acquisition reversal or full clearance for it. Production baseline `76998a6` stays unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-remaining-20261022/`.

The owned `ROOM2080.RDT` and its checksum are pinned above. Procedure `17:2`, RDT file offset 5550, copies script variable 4 through variable 16, adds one and compares variable 4 with literal fifty before conditionally scheduling `17:3`. That procedure invokes `Aot_on` slot 14 at RDT file offset 5604. The decoded source contains no `Evt_exec`, `Evt_chain`, `Gosub` or event-interaction target for `17:2`. Slot 3 instead starts as a message interaction with payload `00 00 00 00 00 FF`. These decoded references are preserved in `source-dispatch.json`. Unreferenced decoded code is a lead, not a complete proof against opaque native or indirect dispatch, nor a proof that the item can never be reached spatially.

Native interaction reaches PS1 virtual PC `0x8004E3F4`, the message handler, with CPU register A0 pointing to the same six-byte payload at `0x8011BA2A`. Its descriptor is at `0x8011BA1E`. The pinned handler at `0x8004E3F4..0x8004E428` passes the payload to message setup at `0x8002F230`. The separate event-interaction handler at `0x8004E42C..0x8004E468` calls event scheduling at `0x8004FAA0`. This local source inspection does not establish the proposed message-to-counter link.

Read-only message breakpoints and counter-write watchpoints record three batches of 38, 29 and 29 message-handler entries over thirty, twenty and twenty seconds. The first two batches are one continuous visit; the third follows ordinary departure and reentry. All 96 entries identify the same slot 3 payload. Script variable 4 at PS1 virtual `0x800CC920`, unsigned little-endian width two, mask `0xFFFF`, reads two before each trace, at every recorded stop and after each trace. Seventeen additional write-watchpoint stops in the native copy routine also retain two; this is not a claim that the address receives no writes. ID 159 at `0x800CC9F8`, unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0x00000001`, remains clear. `message-traces.jsonl` preserves each observation. The earlier fifty-interaction activation hypothesis is not reproduced by these ordinary messages; the procedure's general reachability remains unresolved.

In every accepted current-source capture, slot 14's pointer at PS1 virtual `0x800C6700`, unsigned little-endian width four, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`, resolves to the type 80, amount one, ID 159 descriptor. Its interaction rectangle remains X/Z `20000/20000`, width/depth `1000/1000`. This outlying placement suggests a placeholder, but does not establish absence. ### Out-of-bounds pickup diagnostic, 2026-10-22

Status: Confirmed for the two position-assisted native ID 159 acquisitions, their inventory changes, settled clearance, ordinary departure/reentry after collection and the three stated F1 loads on the same pinned SLUS-00421 executable and DuckStation version. These pickups occur outside the playable collision enclosure. They are diagnostic evidence, not confirmation of normal-game activation, ordinary availability or campaign readiness. Baseline `ce22a33` remains unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-reachability-20261022/`.

The complete owned catalog has only one ID 159 creator, at `ROOM2080.RDT` file offset 5384. No decoded bank 8/34 write supplies a separate acquisition alias. A conservative decoded graph rooted at `16:0`, `17:0` and `17:1` includes explicit calls, scheduled events and event-interaction targets. Procedures `17:2` and `17:3` remain unreferenced, alongside four other procedures. This extends the earlier direct-reference lead but does not exclude every native or indirect invocation. `script-graph.json` preserves the graph and its limits. The existing negative creator proof cannot remove this item: its creator actually runs, and its descriptor really exists.

An independent external lead is BioRand's [PC room catalog at commit `bef6a38261ee877cd36b52c9c76831e36290e1ee`](https://github.com/biorand/classic/blob/bef6a38261ee877cd36b52c9c76831e36290e1ee/IntelOrca.Biohazard.BioRand/data/re2/rdt.json), room `208`, item slot 14. It overrides that slot's type to zero. The same commit's [node construction](https://github.com/biorand/classic/blob/bef6a38261ee877cd36b52c9c76831e36290e1ee/IntelOrca.Biohazard.BioRand/DoorRandomiser.cs) applies the override and omits type-zero rows from the randomization pool. Neither location explains the exclusion. BioRand targets the PC Sourcenext release, so this establishes omission from its randomizer pool only. It does not prove that the original PC or PS1 pickup is unused or unobtainable. `external-lead.json` records URLs, commit, interpretation and local checksums; no external item override is imported into the tracker.

The source collision data supplies four surrounding box walls. In a copied-RAM Unicorn experiment, the pinned native collision routine at PS1 virtual `0x80048C68` blocks four small boundary-crossing requests with radius 450, floor mask one and native mask `0x01008000`. An artificial jump from X/Z `-15000/-20000` to `20000/20000` passes through unchanged. These are isolated routine executions, not DuckStation gameplay or evidence of a legal large movement. The result rejects a wall-rectangle-only absence rule: a full movement/activation argument is still needed. `isolated-collision-checks.json` keeps both the blocked cases and the counterexample. No production geometric exclusion follows.

For the new native diagnostic, boot the prior private `use-after-native.sav`, SHA256 `a560c6e143cd2e68834c021d58b0f41b6f7281034f1cf90656ef5ae41283c463`. The initial RAM again matches the earlier follow-up byte for byte. After native free control, prepare X/Z `19900/20500`, yaw zero, without changing height or floor. The 620-unit native forward point then falls inside the parked `20000/20000`, width/depth `1000/1000` interaction area. Several file descriptors overlap it. Native input first delivers file IDs 125, 126 and 127 from slots 6, 7 and 8; they remain excluded from completion by the existing file policy. Further native confirmation collects the distinct slot 14 item. This is not the ordinary message interaction or the previously collected ID 209.

All following memory addresses are PS1 virtual, little-endian. ID 159 at `0x800CC9F8`, unsigned width four, mask `0x00000001`, changes from clear to set. Inventory slot 5 at `0x800CCB7C/0x800CCB7D`, unsigned one-byte type/quantity, each mask `0xFF`, gains type 80, quantity one. Slot 14's pointer at `0x800C6700`, unsigned width four, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`, still identifies type 80, amount one, ID 159; its descriptor's unsigned SCE byte changes from two to zero. The native selected-item pointer at `0x800C6748`, same pointer width/order/mask, distinguishes the preliminary files from ID 159. File IDs 125/126/127 use `0x800CC9F4`, unsigned width four, masks `0x00000004/0x00000002/0x00000001`. These four IDs are the only changed acquisition bits; the other inventory entries remain byte-identical. Script variable 4 at `0x800CC920`, unsigned width two, full mask `0xFFFF`, stays two. Collection therefore does not establish the proposed counter activation.

The settled tracker changes from two Taken out of three and Incomplete to three Taken and Cleared. Return the player to X/Z `-15800/-23317`, yaw zero, after free control; clearance remains. Ordinary departure through the known `01-08`/`01-07` pair and reentry retain Taken/Cleared. The earlier native F1 load removes the inventory item, reverses the file/acquisition bits and returns Incomplete. Repeating the same assisted positioning and native pickup again sets ID 159 and adds the inventory item. Its first captured acknowledgement still returns Retry; closing it yields settled Cleared. The later F1 load restores the acquired in-bounds checkpoint, and another earlier F1 load reverses it again. Both earlier restores and the later restore reproduce their independently extracted checkpoint RAM exactly.

This closes the diagnostic item-reading, collection, clearance and rewind checks for ID 159 under the stated assistance. ### Stage and entry audit, 2026-10-22

Status: Candidate for ordinary-play reachability on the same SLUS-00421 revision. This bounded source audit and replay of existing captures supply no new gameplay observation or unused-item confirmation. Production baseline `804b5f2` remains unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-entrypoints-20261022/`.

Owned `COMMON/BIN/STAGE2.BIN` is 1626 bytes, SHA256 `d519788cd278f83f75e9c902a97c3626c8f515e41ac2c1c17e0c76bb640fa0bc`. The complete file matches guest RAM at PS1 virtual `0x8011A000` in the prior `use-entry`, `diagnostic-before` and `acquired-reentered` captures, whose full-RAM checksums are recorded above. These are reused native observations across inventory use and reentry, not three new executions of the initializer. Its entry at `0x8011A004..0x8011A050` writes data pointers `0x8011A054`, `0x8011A3D4` and `0x8011A594` to `0x800C7C58`, `0x800C66B0` and `0x800C66B4` respectively. Each destination is a four-byte unsigned little-endian field, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`, and all three retain those values in each capture. The entry has one direct call, to `0x8003148C`, and no direct call to the script constructor, scheduler or item-activation handler. Bytes after the entry are pointed-to data; disassembling their coincidental instruction encodings does not establish additional executable paths. This checks the stage-entry lead, not every possible native callback.

Across all 125 owned source catalogs, the decoded regular/four-point door declarations targeting `01-08` are `ROOM2070.RDT` offset 3894, slot 2, and `ROOM2080.RDT` offset 4954, slot 30. Their destination X/Y/Z/yaw values are `-15462/0/-15336/-3067` and `-21095/0/-15462/2992`. Both destinations lie inside the enclosing walls identified in the prior diagnostic. The second trigger itself is parked at X/Z `20000/20000`, width/depth one. An additional bytewise scan for complete packets throughout every declared procedure range, including bytes skipped by decoded flow, finds only those same two leads. These are RDT file offsets and signed little-endian 16-bit destination operands, mask `0xFFFF`. This bounds the inspected door declarations; it does not rule out arbitrary native teleports or establish a legal movement bound.

The room's decoded slot 3 writes are its initial message at offset 5056 and two resets in unreferenced procedure `17:4`, offsets 5608 and 5628. Those resets disable the slot and then make it an event targeting `17:4` itself. Neither targets the counter procedure `17:2`. Slot 14 has only its creator at 5384 and the sole `Aot_on` reference at 5604 in unreferenced `17:3`; no decoded reset or replacement moves its parked rectangle. Rebuilding the conservative invocation graph reproduces the same six unreferenced procedures from the prior audit.

An aligned scan of the complete pinned executable body finds the same three direct calls to constructor `0x8004FA54` and five to scheduler `0x8004FAA0` listed in `audit.json`. It finds no aligned literal pointer or ADDIU/ORI low-immediate construction for either address. This extends the direct-call lead to those simple pointer forms only; arbitrary arithmetic-derived pointers and other program-counter writers remain outside that scan. Neither this result nor the stage/door checks closes the physical-reachability limit. ID 159 remains required in production. No exclusion, new runtime-source coverage, emulator boot, guest-memory write, UI change or new external review is claimed.

The source audit, 329 local documentation links, whitespace and preservation of all eight unrelated worktree files pass. This evidence-only contribution changes no production code or interpretation rule.

### Native walking and running bounds, 2026-10-22

Status: Confirmed for the observed walking/running collision stops and two exact native restores on the same SLUS-00421 executable and DuckStation version. Permanent item absence remains Candidate. The owned `PL0/PLD/PL00W00.PLW` is 32928 bytes, SHA256 `46662f13ce2f2111ab74917fae7d124822f8cf9ad9650c14bd5b8a536dff854d`. Its complete sequence and motion sections match the starting guest RAM, through pointers at PS1 virtual `0x800C7EA8` and `0x800C7EA4`, respectively. Both pointer fields are four-byte unsigned little-endian, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`. They resolve to `0x801D9508` and `0x801D9B84`. The matched sections are 1660 and 27848 bytes, SHA256 `0b65b907fb8836222dea62b460595981d420473097b1222cf9e9b6111cc3a8c6` and `c7fcec69bef776c859d519f06b24c66f216690f528bd8a6f1a7ec8039e135043`. Native step extraction at `0x80015880..0x800159E8` derives signed halfword X/Y/Z deltas from motion positions. `animation_audit.py` decodes the listed walking/running sequences in thirteen complete owned `PL00W*.PLW` animation files. Six additional files have the exact short stub layout and supply no such motion bound. `sources.json` records each file identity and checksum. The maximum unadjusted component magnitudes across the inspected sequences are `251/0/20`; these are data-derived values, not a global player-speed cap. The loaded section's 171 selected frames also agree exactly with isolated execution of the pinned native extractor over copied RAM. Those synthetic frame/sequence seeds are not DuckStation writes or new gameplay observations. Backward, special movement, costumes, arbitrary pose transitions and other native position writers are outside this bound.

In new hidden gameplay, native walking stops at the north wall. Native running is then traced at all four enclosing walls, with 260 complete before/after collision pairs and 248 pairs that correct the requested X/Z. Breakpoints are at `0x800334A0` before the collision wrapper and `0x80033510` after its collision call. All accepted running observations use the native running routine bytes `01 02 01 00`, floor zero, actor flag mask `0x8` clear and collision-owner value zero. The observed component magnitudes reach `234/0/19`, agreeing with the loaded running sequence and its native seventeen-unit X reduction. This checks actual running input, rather than inferring it from a key command.

Floor at `0x800C7E2A` is one unsigned byte, mask `0xFF`. Game-held input at `0x800C647C` is unsigned little-endian width four, with forward/run mask `0x201` set in the accepted running traces. ### Native wall actions and object gates, 2026-10-22

Status: Confirmed for the five observed action attempts on the same SLUS-00421 executable and DuckStation version. The source and native-code inspection below bounds specific exceptional-movement leads only. Permanent item absence remains Candidate. Production baseline `4982ac4` is unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-special-20261022/`.

The pinned interaction dispatch table at PS1 virtual `0x8009F87C` contains unsigned little-endian four-byte function pointers, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`. Entry 8 calls `0x8004E4F4..0x8004E558`, which adds three signed little-endian halfwords, mask `0xFFFF`, from its interaction payload to the selected actor's X/Y/Z and sets actor flag `0x00020000`. It contains no inline collision check. The room's decoded declarations and resets contain no interaction type 8. A bytewise scan throughout every declared procedure finds one apparent complete type-8 reset packet at RDT offset 5885. It begins inside the operand of `Item_lost` at 5884, followed by `Aot_on` at 5886, and supplies no decoded reset. This rejects that direct source lead, not arbitrary native activation or every way to mutate a descriptor.

All four decoded object declarations have kind zero and flag operand `0x000A`. The pinned constructor at `0x800518E0` sets flags to the sign-extended operand OR one when the prior flags word is zero, and otherwise clears the flags word. The declarations' six signed little-endian halfword operands at packet offsets 26 through 36 are all zero, each mask `0xFFFF`. The four decoded `Pos_set` instructions at RDT offsets 5734, 5772, 6432 and 6822 all follow `Work_set` selecting kind 4, index 4, which the pinned handler resolves to object 4. No decoded packet uses either native member-write opcode, `0x34` or `0x35`. These are bounded decoded-source statements, not a proof about arbitrary native writers.

Native object pushing additionally requires object flag `0x80`; the inspected kind-2 update path is separate. Support lookup at `0x80036538` requires flags masked with `0x101` to equal `0x101` and rejects flag `0x2`. Native object collision at `0x80034CF4` also rejects object flag `0x2`. All five records in the starting and four subsequent captures have kind zero, neither flag `0x80` nor `0x100`, and flag `0x2` whenever active. The stale intervening record remains inactive. This closes the inspected push/support lead for those records, rather than assuming an object position write could never affect the player. Records start at PS1 virtual `0x800C8450`, stride `0x1F8`; flags are unsigned little-endian four-byte values at offset zero, tested masks `0x1`, `0x2`, `0x80` and `0x100`. Kind is one unsigned byte at offset eight, mask `0xFF`. The player's collision-owner field remains zero in every capture.

New hidden gameplay holds native action and forward input at each previously established wall stop. Read-only breakpoints trace entry `0x800353C4` and the actual idle/walk returns at `0x80039B84` and `0x80039E68`. All 268 complete entry/return pairs return zero, with unchanged X/Y/Z, floor zero, collision owner zero and actor flag `0x8` clear. The action argument is four and game-held input at `0x800C647C`, unsigned little-endian width four, has action mask `0x80` throughout and forward/action mask `0x81` during each attempt. Pose, routine and collision fields use the widths/order pinned in the preceding movement follow-up. The routine bytes remain in the observed idle/walk states; no special state is claimed.

### Native task reset and auxiliary dispatch, 2026-10-23

Status: Confirmed for the two observed room-reset calls, scoped pointer construction and exact native restore on the same SLUS-00421 executable and DuckStation version. The deliberate active-task counterexample below is isolated copied-RAM execution, not gameplay. Permanent item absence remains Candidate. Production baseline `93b8fa6` is unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-activation-calls-20261023/`.

This follows a specific indirect-activation hypothesis: a retained task instruction pointer could enter `17:2` despite having no caller in the room's decoded graph. Native reset at `0x8004F8A4` clears the active and nesting bytes on ten room tasks. The loop ends at `0x8004F8EC`, leaving their old instruction pointers untouched. Main dispatch at `0x8004FF60` checks activity before reading or executing the pointed-to bytecode. Room tasks start at PS1 virtual `0x800CF8D8`, stride `0x174`, slots zero through nine. Auxiliary slots ten through thirteen start at `0x800D0760` and use the separate loop at `0x80013BD4`. Active, nesting and slot-index fields are unsigned bytes at task offsets one, two and three, each mask `0xFF`. The instruction pointer at task offset `0x1C` is unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`.

`reset_probe.py` seeds all ten copied room contexts active with the same pointer to the counter procedure, `0x8011BC0A`. Without reset, the dispatcher reaches that pointer and the probe stops before executing its bytecode. After executing the exact native reset, all ten active bytes are zero; the stale pointers remain, but the dispatcher executes no bytecode. Counter 4 stays unchanged in both cases. These deliberately constructed copied-RAM states test the reset gate without writing task activity, task pointers or counters to DuckStation. They do not validate reset timing or every later writer by themselves.

New native departure from `01-08` into `01-07`, then return into `01-08`, reaches both reset breakpoints. Each after-loop observation has ten inactive, nesting-zero room tasks and the same old pointers as before the loop. All ten were already inactive before each observed reset, so no native one-to-zero clearing is claimed. Auxiliary task records are unchanged across each reset and some remain active. The scoped traces then verify 22 complete constructor entry/return pairs, thirteen during departure and nine during reentry. They show initialization of the resident init/main procedures and subsequent main procedure one scheduling. The return trace additionally verifies three complete `Gosub` entry/post-write pairs; the departure trace does not instrument `Gosub`.

Constructor `0x8004FA54` and `Gosub` handler `0x80050A44` form the script pointer as the selected table base plus its unsigned little-endian halfword entry at twice the procedure index, mask `0xFFFF`. The shared table field at PS1 virtual `0x800D0D34` is unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`; bank-mode field `0x800D0D40` is an unsigned byte, mask `0xFF`. Each observed result matches that calculation and the corresponding owned procedure table. An explicit constructor starts auxiliary slot ten while the mode byte is still zero, so mode alone cannot identify the script bank. The audit uses table identity, context slot and resident init/main offsets rather than the changing numeric room field alone.

The native return path does call procedure two, but from auxiliary table `0x801A1008`, yielding `0x801A1096`. This differs from the room's counter procedure at `0x8011BC0A`. The other two observed auxiliary calls select eight and seven, yielding `0x801A120C` and `0x801A11E0`. Neither constructor nor `Gosub` in the successful traces selects the room counter or its activation procedure at `0x8011BC34`. This rejects the apparent procedure-number alias in these transitions, not every indirect activation path.

The 8192-byte auxiliary-entry snapshot at PS1 virtual `0x801A1000` has SHA256 `f1e06620436ef98819b81b44b2d7aac12af8bfa92bcc4ada347c4fb154e3b6fd`. Its complete 540-byte script matches owned `COMMON/DOOR/DOOR00.DO2`, 56784 bytes, SHA256 `604345d12d0dff0c921183476e2ad2a054c2907a25f51757f227a0471b4a53ce`. The script begins at file offset 20488 and ends before 21028, SHA256 `a3679ff17097ce8ee4e4500f60f18698d93ea7825a3abcd49a3c15a9338db68c`. The two unsigned little-endian four-byte header offsets at file offset 20480 are 548 and 2992, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`; the native snapshot relocates them to `0x801A1224` and `0x801A1BB0`. An initial assumption that this section began at file offset zero finds no match and is rejected. A raw full-script scan across all 55 owned DO2 files finds this sole match. Full model/asset relocation is outside this comparison.

`auxiliary_source_audit.py` decodes the matched table's nine procedures with the pinned opcode widths. The three native caller/target pairs match its exact source packets and table entries. It contains no decoded item-creator, `Aot_on`, direct-award or `0x44` packet from the inspected table-changing lead. That bounded opcode absence does not establish what every other native handler can do. No production source interpreter is extended.

Reproduce from `use-after-native.sav`, whose checksum is pinned above. After native free control, prepare `01-08` at X/Z `-15462/-15336`, yaw 3077, walk forward briefly and use the existing Cross macro. Trace reset entry/after-loop, constructor entry and its actual return addresses. Return from `01-07` at `-15084/-13807`, yaw 512; also trace `Gosub` entry and post-write `0x80050AA4`. The first armed trace receives no input and times out with zero observations. A first return attempt records 635 constructor-only stops but no reset or passage; native play displaces the player and health drops by thirty. It is excluded from completed-transition evidence. Reprepare after native free control and hold the native action across a three-second delayed resume. The successful return supplies the scoped results above. Three preparations write only the established nine X/Z/facing fields.

The four settled captures are starting `7527d22225b732c1a8b7551ffe064b91d5639df6124fbbf0a8a0b17f50e028a9`, departed `914ab492069bc44e14e4d0214a51b7b11f40b65027cb0a9e14541de62df98902`, reentered `236d09fa7f051a1fac311fddfc8c17f65bcdada929f1e65dee812d3375cf0461` and an actual paused F1 restore identical to the starting RAM. Four fresh trackers first establish the starting visit and reconstruct each capture. The target room remains Incomplete with two Taken and ID 159 Available while current or source-established Uncollected after departure. Acquisition banks, all eight inventory records and counter 4 remain unchanged. Health is `28890/28890/28860/28890`, with the last value restored by F1. This adds no pickup, clearance or memory-card persistence evidence.

The single isolated boot and load preparation keep achievements disabled, per-game overrides absent, cards None/None and audio muted. A suppressed capture verifies the black display. No new health/ammunition aid or item, inventory, story, mode, control, height, floor, task, instruction-pointer or script-table patch is applied to DuckStation. `cleanup.json` verifies both helpers, the owned emulator and GDB listener stopped, with isolated settings and selected slot restored byte for byte. User state is untouched. Raw RAM, owned assets, code excerpts, checkpoints and game imagery remain private. Runtime-source coverage remains thirty; no UI changes.

The task-reset timing and correctly scoped auxiliary calls close these specific carry-over/alias leads. They do not prove every future callback, position writer or story state, and do not settle ordinary-play completion membership. ID 159 stays required in production.

### Auxiliary native write audit, 2026-10-23

Status: Candidate interpretation from exact native code executed in constructed copied RAM. This adds no gameplay observation or permanent-absence confirmation. The executable, owned auxiliary source, entry snapshot and emulator version are pinned in the preceding follow-up. Production baseline `b5f8a11` is unchanged. Private evidence is under `/tmp/re-map-re2-aux-writes-20261023/`.

The remaining concrete auxiliary lead is opcode `0x4D`, native handler `0x80014720`. Its actor-relative store at offset `0x1C` could be mistaken for a task instruction-pointer write. The actual target comes from a separate animation-record pointer array at PS1 virtual `0x800CCE58`. These pointers are unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0xFFFFFFFF`. The matched source selects records zero through two, at `0x801BD24C`, `0x801BD398` and `0x801BD4E4`, stride `0x14C`. They do not overlap the room or auxiliary task contexts. The door-animation control pointer is at `0x800BBE80`, with the same width, byte order and full mask. An initial `0x800CBE80` calculation is rejected; the native signed immediate `-0x4180` is added to `0x800C0000`.

The handler initializes animation fields and calls `0x800146BC`, then rendering-packet builder `0x8002BFDC`. A repeated model uses copy routine `0x800104B0`. The actor's `+0x1C` field receives a model-data pointer. The selected auxiliary task's separate `+0x1C` field advances by the source packet's 22-byte width. No scheduler or room-task constructor occurs in these executions. The source's `0x0800` flag can set the door-animation control halfword at `+0x248`; that is not a task-activity field. Actor/control halfwords are unsigned little-endian with full mask `0xFFFF`; source position halfwords are signed little-endian and expand into signed four-byte actor fields.

`handler_probe.py` starts from the preceding settled RAM and overlays the captured 8192-byte auxiliary entry buffer. It executes native initialization ranges `0x80013800` through before `0x80013890`, `0x800138D0` through before `0x800138E8`, and `0x80013920` through before `0x8001398C`. The already-relocated snapshot supplies the second range's explicit pool-pointer and control-pointer register inputs. GPU setup and raw-file relocation are not repeated. Code hooks assert each stopping boundary; an initial interval-end attempt that stops short of its expected boundary is rejected. The task pointer and auxiliary table/mode are explicit copied-RAM setup values. None of this construction writes to DuckStation.

Execute the two exact source packets in procedure one and the three in procedure two, then repeat each sequence to exercise tagged rendering-data reuse. All ten calls return one, advance only the selected task pointer and match the source-selected actor/model fields. Six calls use the copy branch. Models zero and one have twelve and thirty-three triangles. Rendering output begins at `0x801A1BB0` and ends at `0x801A37D0` or `0x801A4C70` for the two repeated sequences, separate from script bytes and task records. Every observed write fits the asserted stack, selected animation record, control record, model tag, rendering output or selected task-pointer range.

Byte comparisons preserve all ten room-task records, the shared script table/mode, player state, item registry, target descriptor, acquisition banks, inventory and counter 4 across every handler call. These checks reject this specific handler as a cross-table activation mechanism for the exact matched packets. They do not cover arbitrary operands, every other native handler, future story states or physical reachability. No new emulator boot, load, aid, tracker visit or UI change occurs. The reader and ID 159's required completion check remain unchanged.

Reproduce with `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/re-map-re2-scripted-exclusions-20261022/deps python3 /tmp/re-map-re2-aux-writes-20261023/handler_probe.py`. `handler-probe.json` records the source offsets, selected records, output bounds, native callees, copy branches and limits. Owned bytes and raw RAM remain private.

## RE2-COLLECT-010: system-mode predicate gates alternate pickup identities

Status: Candidate for SLUS-00421. Owned-source inspection and a hypothetical fixed-flag proof explain a possible cause of ID 116's unresolved check. Neither mode-bit-clear gameplay nor the flag's lifecycle is confirmed. No production condition or exclusion changes follow from this finding.

Follow-up, 2026-10-22: [RE2-COLLECT-011](#re2-collect-011-pinned-native-mode-excludes-an-alternate-pickup) below establishes the observed bit-set initializer/context, enforces that context in the profile and verifies the resulting exclusion and clearance. It supersedes the unresolved bit-set-profile limit here. The flag's named setting, opposite-mode gameplay and exhaustive native-writer coverage remain unconfirmed.

Game, executable and emulator are pinned in [RE2-COLLECT-009](#re2-collect-009-native-pickup-and-use-keep-acquisition-identities-separate). Private evidence shares `/tmp/re-map-re2-activation-20261022/`, using production commit `e54e4d3`. Owned `ROOM2020.RDT`, source `01-02`, is 167836 bytes with SHA256 `6ee69fee262b4390ac5f4ecf36c54335cd7f6fa1b3b14af71720925bcb54e8a9`. All offsets below are in that RDT unless prefixed as PS1 virtual addresses.

Procedure `17:0` checks bank 0 bit 25 at offset 7366. Its bit-clear branch creates a non-item interaction in slot 12; the opposite branch creates type 20, amount fifteen, ID 115 in slot 12 at 7394. A separate bit-clear check at 7422 gates slot 15's creators at 7434 and 7460. Both declare type 20, amount fifteen, ID 116. An internal bank 1 bit 1 choice selects between those two creators without changing ID 116. The full decoded candidate list is complete. These are distinct acquisition IDs whose creator branches depend on the same system flag.

Bank 0 bit 25 is mask `0x00000040` at PS1 virtual `0x800C7CE4`, unsigned little-endian width four. It is also the existing native pickup-quantity override mask in [RE2-QUANTITY-001](#re2-quantity-001-native-pickup-award-amount-differs-from-catalog). That reuse does not establish a stable presence predicate. The production presence contract currently permits bank/bit pairs `1/0`, `1/1`, `1/6` and `4/7`; it does not include this system flag. Production fixed-scenario exclusions use the separate required scenario mask, not an assumption that this mode bit is fixed.

The private proof supplies the existing mandatory scenario predicates plus one explicit hypothetical fixed bank 0 bit 25 value to `scenario_absent_items`. With value zero it returns absent IDs 115 and 249. With value one it returns absent IDs 116 and 249. `mode-proof-verification.json` independently rebuilds the decoded source and reproduces `mode-absence-lead.json`. This is an experiment with an added assumption, not a new metadata export, live proof, supported second mode or general absence rule. The flag must have an established lifetime and an explicit profile/runtime contract before this result could justify removing a completion check.

Read-only replay of the preceding pass's `other-before`, `other-three-accepted`, `other-earlier-final-restored` and `other-later-restored` captures finds this system bit set, ID 116 clear and its slot 15 pointer zero. ID 115 acquisition reverses through the earlier/later pair as previously documented. These are reused observations from [RE2-COLLECT-008](#re2-collect-008-scenario-exclusions-through-audited-script-effects), not new gameplay. ID 116 uses PS1 virtual `0x800CC9F4`, unsigned little-endian width four, mask `0x00000800`; slot 15's pointer is at `0x800C6704`, same width/order, full mask `0xFFFFFFFF`. The shared scenario word remains separately pinned. Missing slot 15 alone still proves no absence.

A static executable scan identifies a candidate direct-offset writer at PS1 virtual `0x8002A944`. Within the function starting at `0x8002A91C`, an `lw` from `0x800C7CE4`, an `ori` with immediate `0x40` at `0x8002A93C`, and the store at `0x8002A944` form a set-bit lead. The scan does not establish when that function runs, its callers or every possible writer. An initial disassembly stopped at undecodable bytes and was rerun with skip-data handling. An initial substring match also accepted immediate `0x4000` and is rejected; the retained scan requires the exact `0x40` immediate. `system40-writer-leads.json`, SHA256 `7e3d82b64d694c3d7f59734efb15a3c47329a0bb00b88049a20a9118e6a0a488`, preserves the bounded result and these limits. This is not complete dataflow or runtime writer evidence.

The finding does not assign a difficulty, region or named game-mode meaning to the flag. It does not prove ID 116 impossible in the user's supported profile, validate the opposite branch in gameplay, resolve full clearance in `01-02`, or alter the remaining ID 159 case. The new native pass in RE2-COLLECT-009 observes only the same bit-set state. Its controlled setup and cleanup apply here; no mode bit is patched and no additional runtime source is claimed.

Contributions:

- 2026-10-22; human user; requested continuation of the remaining RE2 tracking investigation.
- 2026-10-22; Codex root, exact verified model `gpt-6.1-sol`; inspected both creator branches, reproduced the hypothetical fixed-flag proofs, checked four prior native captures, retained the limited executable writer lead and authored this Candidate finding.
